75 mg at bed time and drug B 120 mg/ 5 ml (300 ml sol.) 150 mg at bed time.
Calculate the volume of each drug dispensed for 4 weeks.

-If a drug has an oral bioavailability of 50%, what would be the required oral dose
to match the systemic exposure of a 40 mg IV dose?
Oral Dose = IV Dose / Bioavailability
Since the BA is 50% (0.5), the calculation is:
Oral Dose = 40 mg / 0.5 = 80 mg
6-A patient is prescribed Lugol’s solution 5% iodine, 0.3 mL TDS. How
much iodine is provided each day?
Answer:
0.3 mL × 3 = 0.9 mL/day
5 g in 100 mL → X in 0.9 mL
X = (5 ÷ 100) × 0.9 = 0.045 g iodine/day = 45 mg
17-Lithium acetate (LiC₂H₃O₂) has an atomic weight of Li = 6.95, acetate =
59.044 g/mol. How many millimoles of lithium are in a 300 mg tablet?
Answer:
Molecular weight = 6.95 + 59.044 = 65.994 g/mol
300 mg ÷ 65.994 = 4.546 mmol of lithium acetate
Lithium content: (6.95 ÷ 65.994) × 300 mg = 31.59 mg Li
Millimoles of lithium = 31.5937 ÷ 6.95 = 4.546 mmol
18-A sulfonamide eye drop formulation contains 0.002 g of phenylmercuric
nitrate in 100 mL. If this preservative is available as a 3 mg in 10 mL
solution, how much is needed to prepare 15 mL of the eye drops?
Answer:
Required: (0.002 ÷ 100) × 15 = 0.0003 g = 0.3 mg
3 mg in 10 mL → 0.3 mg = (0.3 ÷ 3) × 10 = 1 mL
19-The half-life of digoxin in normal patients is 36 hours. In nephropathic
patients, it is 4.5 days. If no loading dose is used, how long will it take to
reach steady state in a nephropathic patient?
Answer:
t½ = 4.5 days = 108 hours
Steady state = 4–5 t½ → average: 4.5 × 4.5 = 20.25 days20-A prescription of 100 ml of 1% chlorohexidine base and you only have

Q: How much of 20% K₂Cr₂O₇·2H₂O (MW = 273), H₂O = 18 × 2 = 36 must be used to produce
50 mL of the anhydrous K₂Cr₂O₇?
Solution:
20% w/v means:
20 g → 100 mL
So:
x → 50 mL
→ x = 10 g of K₂Cr₂O₇·2H₂O (hydrated form)
Now convert hydrated to anhydrous:
MW of K₂Cr₂O₇·2H₂O = 273
MW of anhydrous K₂Cr₂O₇ = 273 − 36 = 237
273 g → 237 g
10 g → x
→ x = (10 × 237) ÷ 273
→ x = 8.7 g of anhydrous K₂Cr₂O₇

gluconate

Ans:elemental iron (Fe) = 300 mg – 265 mg = 35 mgfe%=(35/300)*100=11.667 %

12-If a drug has an oral bioavailability of 50%, what would be the required oral dose to match
the systemic exposure of a 40 mg IV dose?
Oral Dose = IV Dose / Bioavailability
Since the BA is 50% (0.5), the calculation is:
Oral Dose = 40 mg / 0.5 = 80 mg

258)Surfactant definition
surfactant, substance such as a detergent that, when added to a liquid,
reduces its surface tension, thereby
increasing its spreading and wetting properties. In the dyeing of textiles,

349-Drug given as 1 gm per 6 hours for 3 days.
The question is if 150 mg is equal to 2500000 units of Amoxicillin/Ampicillin, then how much units
are needed for the whole treatment.
Options: 2 million units or 20 million units.
Dose: 1 gm x 4 times x 3 days = 12 gm => 12000 mg
since 150 mg = 2500000 units
12000 mg = x units? => x = 2500000 x 12000/150 = 20 million units.

 Most common metabolic pathway------ oxidation.
 Glucuronidation is the most common type of conjugation pathways
 Phase II biotransformation reaction----- acetylation.
 Which one is phase II metabolism------- Sulfate conjugation.
 Not form of biotransformation: esterification (not phase II).
 Metabolism of drugs OR Cyp enzyme metabolism site occurs at the subcellularorgan in ER
(smooth), mitochondria and ribosome.

Part5 : Stereochemistry
 *Stereochemistry purpose: to find relationship between molecules have the sameatoms.
 *StereoSelective rxn: produce Specific Stereoisomers in high amount.(produce onestereoisomer in122
higher amount than the other example S more than R)
 Isomer: Two compounds with same molecular formula ( same weight )
 Chiral=Stereocenter : If carbon atom attached to four different groups (asymmetric carbon
center)
 Achiral : if the groups repeated or similar
 Optical active compound: ability to rotate to light
- Clockwise (+) called Dextrorotatory D
- Anticlockwise (-) called levorotatory L
Optically active: Lactic acid, Levofloxacin, levodopa, carvedilol, warfarin,
nifedipine
Optically inactive: pyruvate, Racemic
 Meso compound:
- compound has plane or center of symmetry (internal mirror image )
- optically inactive contains two chiral center
Enantiomers:
- same chemical & same physical
- mirror image but not superimposed
- different pharmacological
- at least one chiral
 Diastereomers:
- Different physical & different chemical
- Not mirror image not superimposed
- At least two chiral centers
 Racemic mixture :
- Mixture have two enantiomers (Dextrorotatory&Levorotatory) 50/50 =
equimolar
- Same chemical and different physical
- Optically inactive except warfarin : active
- Ex: Warfarin, verapamil, CCBS except(nifedipine), ofloxacin, ibuprofen,
omeprazole, ketamine, salbutamol, propoxyphene, salmetrol, formetrol,
citalopram, cetrizine
- NOT RACEMIC: naproxen, esmoprazole, nifedipine123
 Bioisosteres : groups with same chemical , physical and biological EX: coumarins
 R&S isomer : Absolute configuration
- R ------ clockwise to the right
- S ------- anticlockwise to the left
 Types of isomers:
1. Structural = constitutional
2. Stereoisomer
- Enantiomers
- Diastereomers
Types of Diastereomers: Geometric, conformational, mesocompound, Epimer
 Epimer : type of Diastereomers Not mirror and one chiral only
 Isomer :
- stereoisomers have same connectivity
- constitutional = structural isomers NOT have same connectivity
EX1: dimethyl ether and ethanol same molecular formula but NOT same
connectivity
EX2:
 Stereoisomers can converted to each other by rotation around single bond
 Chair conformation for ring structure
- Equatorial: in the plane of the ring
- Axial: perpendicular to ring
- Which conformation of cyclohexane is chiral? ------ Equatorial124

Questions:
1. Same physiochemical & Different pharmacological : Enantiomers
2. Same physiochemical & Optical active : Enantiomers
3. Same chemical and different physical & Optical Inactive : Racemic
4. Different physiochemical & Different pharmacological : Diastereomers
5. An asymmetric carbon, aka chiral carbon or stereogenic carbon, is a carbon atom that is bonded to
four different substituents.
6. Isomers have following Properties : same M.Wt , plain of asymmetry & chiral carbons.
7. Meso compound: Optically inactive, Mirror image with at least 2 chiral centers or even number e.g.
2,4,6. Contain an internal mirror image which gives a superimposable image upon rotation.
8. Stereoselective synthesis is used to produce one stereoisomer of a compound with the desired
pharmacological properties while minimizing the formation of unwanted stereoisomers that might have
different, possibly adverse, effects.
9. Stereochemistry knowledge is crucial in many areas of chemistry and biochemistry, including drug
design, as different isomers of a drug can have different therapeutic effects.
10. Isomers are chemical compounds that have identical chemical formulas but differ in properties and the
arrangement of atoms in the molecule.
11. Molecules that rotate the plane polarized light to right are called R isomers. Molecules that rotate the
plane polarized light to the left are said to be S isomers.
12. Rotating the plane of polarized light Dextro- → Right or clockwise / Levo- → Left or anticlockwise
13. Enantiomers: Has 1 chiral center, Mirror image, Not superimposable and Optically Active , Have
same Physicochemical properties but different geometry.
14. Diastereoisomer : Has 2 or more chiral carbon/center , Not a mirror image, Not superimposed,
Optically Active, Have Different geometry & Physicochemical properties.
15. Enantiomers ⇒ similar physical activity but different pharmacological activity, react with any cpd.
16. Diastereomers ⇒ can have different chemical, physical, pharmacological and biological activity.
17. Enantiomers are NOT diastereomers.
18. 4 Chiral carbons (2^n / 2) = 16 stereoisomers, 14 diastereomers.
19. Cis & Trans cpds are Geometrical Isomers, and can be considered diastereomers.
20. Cis- compound has the chemical Gps on the same side while Trans- has them on different sides.
21. Trans compounds are more stable while Cis compounds are more reactive.

Cleaned 135 Q&A; from Screenshots Baby pink highlight • Black questions/options • Red correct answers
1. Q: Buprenorphine and methadone: safe in pregnancy? 
A: None.



2. Q: A patient develops heparin-induced thrombocytopenia (HIT). Which of the following should NOT be given? 
A) Enoxaparin 
B) Fondaparinux 
C) Argatroban 
D) Bivalirudin 
Answer: A) Enoxaparin



3. Q: Least associated with SIADH? 
Options: Lithium, Carbamazepine, Chlorpromazine, Alcohol 
Answer: Lithium



4. Q: Does levothyroxine cause hypokalemia? 
A: No.



5. Q: Newborn screening for phenylketonuria is done by which test? 
A. Heel test 
B. Guthrie test 
C. Phenylketonuria screening test 
D. Mantoux test 
Answer: A. Heel test



6. Q: Warfarin and omeprazole = increased bleeding? 
A: Yes.



7. Q: A locum pharmacist receives a phone call from a lawyer claiming to represent a customer. The lawyer requests medication information prescribed by a medical practitioner for their client. What information can be handed over to the lawyer? 
A. No information at any stage 
B. Any information required after written consent has been given by the patient C. Any information that the lawyer requests 
D. Any information the lawyer requests after confirming the individual is a lawyer E. Any information required upon receipt 
Answer: B. Any information required after written consent has been given by the patient



8. Q: CAP treatment: if amoxicillin, doxycycline, and azithromycin are not options, should ceftriaxone or cefotaxime be chosen? 
A: Yes.



9. Q: Which is accurate for kidney function: serum creatinine, CrCl, or eGFR? 
A: eGFR first, then creatinine clearance.



10. Q: If the rate of absorption increases but the extent of absorption remains the same, what happens to peak concentration? 
A: Peak concentration increases.



Page 1 
11. Q: Ketoacidosis is caused by which diabetes drug class? 
A: SGLT-2 inhibitors. 
Note: Metformin causes lactic acidosis.



12. Q: Least associated mediator in asthma? 
A) Leukotrienes 
B) Histamine 
C) PAF 
D) Serotonin 
Answer: D) Serotonin



13. Q: Which property is required to release drug from a formulation? 
A: Lipophilicity.



14. Q: Which one requires referral to a doctor? 
A. Painful burning in stomach 
B. Bitter taste in mouth 
C. Pain when bending 
D. Difficulty swallowing 
Answer: D. Difficulty swallowing



15. Q: Clarithromycin: which is least correct? 
A: 500 mg once daily.



16. Q: Which NSAID is selective only at low dose / dose-dependent? 
A: Meloxicam.



17. Q: Which is NOT a function of skin? 
Options: Control core body temperature; Maintain healthy bone 
Answer: Maintain healthy bone



18. Q: Which is least likely to cause vomiting? 
A. Opioids 
B. NSAIDs 
C. Aspirin 
D. Metformin 
Answer: B. NSAIDs



19. Q: Which medication is better for management of COVID-19? 
A. Nirmatrelvir/Ritonavir 
B. Dexamethasone 
C. Molnupiravir 
D. Oseltamivir 
Answer: A. Nirmatrelvir/Ritonavir



20. Q: Spironolactone interaction with amitriptyline? 
A: No.



21. Q: Which of the following drugs is NOT commonly associated with worsening renal function? a) NSAIDs 
b) ACE inhibitors 
c) Metformin 
d) Paracetamol 
Answer: d) Paracetamol



Page 2
22. Q: Which cephalosporin is given by IV route? 
Options: Cefazolin, Cefoxitin, Cefalothin, All 
Answer: All



23. Q: Drug of choice for Enterococcus faecalis? 
Options shown: Meropenem, Ampicillin, Vancomycin, All of the above 
Answer shown: Vancomycin and aminopenicillins/ampicillin



24. Q: When should central venous access be used? 
a) Short-term therapy 
b) Peripheral not available 
c) Isotonic solutions 
d) Non-irritant drugs 
e) Irritant drugs 
Answer: e) Irritant drugs



25. Q: A patient requires IV therapy. In which situation is central venous access preferred? A. When peripheral entry is difficult 
B. For isotonic solution 
C. For highly lipid-soluble drugs 
D. For hypertonic solution 
Answer: D. For hypertonic solution



26. Q: Similar action of estrogen and progesterone? 
A) GnRH level 
B) LH and FSH level 
Answer: B) LH and FSH level



27. Q: Donepezil dose? 
A: 5 mg to 10 mg.



28. Q: Which is correct treatment for cold sore on lips? 
a. Self-limiting after 14 days 
b. Famciclovir started within 24 hours 
c. Acyclovir cream is better than oral famciclovir 
d. Other option 
Answer: A and B both



29. Q: Which is NOT a harm reduction / harm minimization program? 
A: Immunization.



30. Q: Erythromycin base dose is 0.25–0.5 g every 6 hours. Which frequency should be selected? Options: Twice daily, Three times daily, Four times daily 
Answer: Four times daily



31. Q: Plasma protein binding is least affected by what? 
Options: Molecular weight, Drug concentration 
Answer: Molecular weight



Page 3
32. Q: Which is least likely to be a side effect of ezetimibe? 
A. Angioedema 
B. Constipation 
C. Myopathy 
D. Rhabdomyolysis 
Answer: B. Constipation



33. Q: Insulin reduces glucose by: 
a) Increasing glycogenolysis 
b) Increasing cellular uptake of carbohydrates, fats, and proteins 
c) Decreasing glucose excretion 
d) Inhibiting gluconeogenesis 
Answer: b) Increasing cellular uptake of carbohydrates, fats, and proteins



34. Q: Which drug is MOST useful for management of metastatic bone pain? 
A. Meloxicam 
B. Fentanyl 
C. Amitriptyline 
D. Carbamazepine 
Answer: B. Fentanyl



35. Q: Acute kidney injury associated with renal calculi is classified as: 
A. Prerenal AKI 
B. Intrinsic AKI 
C. Postrenal AKI 
D. Chronic kidney disease 
Answer: C. Postrenal AKI



36. Q: Fastest onset anesthetic gas? 
Options: Nitrous oxide, Desflurane, Isoflurane, Sevoflurane 
Answer: Nitrous oxide



37. Q: Which counselling statement shows the patient understood levothyroxine counselling? A: Call the doctor if there is fever or sore throat.



38. Q: Pernicious anemia: 
A. Malabsorption of vitamin B9 and vitamin B12 
B. Caused by metformin 
C. Only vitamin B12 
Answer: C. Only vitamin B12



39. Q: Which statement correctly compares the relative concentration / osmolarity / tonicity of dextrose solutions to sodium chloride solutions? 
A) 5% Dextrose (D5W) is isotonic, similar to 0.9% NaCl. 
B) 10% Dextrose is hypotonic compared to 0.45% NaCl. 
C) 0.9% NaCl provides more osmotic strength than 5% dextrose. 
D) 5% Dextrose provides a higher effective osmolarity than 3% NaCl. 
Answer: A



Page 4
40. Q: What combination of antibiotics is commonly used for MRSA? 
A) Rifampicin and Vancomycin 
B) Rifampicin and Ciprofloxacin 
C) Fusidic acid and Vancomycin 
D) Fusidic acid and Rifampicin 
Answer: D) Fusidic acid and Rifampicin



41. Q: Which is NOT typically caused by renal failure? 
A) Hypertension 
B) Hyperkalemia 
C) Hyperphosphatemia 
D) Hyponatremia 
Answer: D) Hyponatremia



42. Q: Which has an active metabolite? 
a) Oxazepam 
b) Morphine 
c) Codeine 
d) Venlafaxine 
Answer note: All have active metabolites except oxazepam. Oxazepam has no active metabolite.



43. Q: Doxepin correct statement? 
1. Side effects appear after one week 
2. Causes diarrhea and bradycardia 
3. Safe to give in postpartum breastfeeding depression 
4. More safe than SSRI in higher doses 
Answer: A / side effects disappear after one week



44. Q: Amiodarone interactions: choose the correct statement. 
Options shown included: 
- Interacts with levothyroxine 
- Controlled hypoglycemia 
- Inhibitor of CYP3A4/5 
- Reduce digoxin dose to one-third 
- Causes QT prolongation with venlafaxine 
Answer shown: Inhibitor of CYP3A4/5; reduce digoxin dose to one-third



45. Q: All are side effects of fentanyl EXCEPT: 
A: Tachycardia.



46. Q: Least addictive is: 
Options: Nicotine, Cocaine, Amphetamine, Codeine, Dihydrocodeine 
Answer shown: Nicotine



47. Q: Agent to lavage gastric content: 
A. Metoclopramide 
B. Magnesium trisilicate and aluminium hydroxide 
Answer: A. Metoclopramide



48. Q: Gentamicin nephrotoxicity will NOT increase with: 
Options: Accompanying nephrotoxic drugs, Increasing age, Increasing duration of treatment, Accompanying ciprofloxacin 
Answer: Accompanying ciprofloxacin



Page 5
49. Q: Least interaction of aspirin with: 
Options: Citalopram, Metoprolol 
Answer: Citalopram



50. Q: Which medicine is least appropriate in pregnancy? 
A. Metoclopramide 
B. Ondansetron 
C. Domperidone 
D. Doxylamine 
Answer: C. Domperidone



51. Q: The osmotic pressure of a 0.1 mol NaCl solution will be approximately how many times that of a 0.1 mol dextrose solution? 
A) 2.0 times 
B) 1.5 times 
C) 0.5 times 
D) 4.0 times 
Answer: A) 2.0 times



52. Q: Codeine in breastfeeding? 
A: Contraindicated.



53. Q: A patient with CKD is admitted to hospital. Which medicine should be continued without interruption? 
A. ACE inhibitor 
B. Metformin 
C. Gliflozin 
D. Rosuvastatin 
Answer: D. Rosuvastatin



54. Q: A pharmacist is reviewing renal drug elimination in relation to urine pH 4.5–7.5. Which drug will have lower renal clearance due to greater reabsorption? 
A) Metronidazole (pKa 2.5) 
B) Atenolol (pKa 9.6) 
C) Furosemide (pKa 3.9) 
D) Morphine (pKa 8.0) 
Answer: A) Metronidazole



55. Q: Amlodipine in angina works mainly by: 
Options: Coronary dilation, Peripheral dilation, Cardiac contractility 
Answer: Coronary dilation



56. Q: Which one is more concerning after the oral glucose tolerance test? 
Options shown: 
- Fasting blood glucose >5 mmol/L 
- After 2 hours >7 mmol/L 
- After 30 minutes >9 mmol/L 
Answer note: After 2 hours more than 7 mmol/L; these ranges were marked correct.



57. Q: Which condition does NOT typically involve synovial inflammation? 
Options shown: Multiple sclerosis, Systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE), Rheumatoid arthritis, Ankylosing spondylitis 
Answer shown: Systemic lupus erythematosus (SLE) / choose SLE first



Page 6
58. Q: Gliclazide is most likely to cause hypoglycemia when used with: 
Options shown: Metformin, Acarbose, Sitagliptin, Pioglitazone 
Answer: Sitagliptin



59. Q: Bacterial vaginosis during pregnancy: 
A. Clindamycin vaginal cream 
B. Oral metronidazole 
C. Fluconazole 
D. Topical azole antifungal 
Answer: A. Clindamycin vaginal cream



60. Q: Drugs used for cardiogenic shock: 
Options shown: Dobutamine, Dopamine, Milrinone 
Answer: All; isoprenaline as well. These are inotropic agents.



61. Q: Which has least cardio side effects / least cardiotoxic? 
Options shown: Mebeverine, Sodium valproate 
Answer: Valproic acid / sodium valproate



62. Q: What should NOT be taken with doxycycline? 
Options: Milk, Zinc 
Answer: Zinc 
Note: Milk/dairy products are okay with doxycycline and minocycline.



63. Q: Rate limiting steps: 
A: SR = dissolution; IR = disintegration.



64. Q: Creatinine clearance (CrCl) depends on what? 
A: Patient's age, weight, gender, and serum creatinine. 
Note: CrCl does not depend on muscle mass directly. Serum creatinine is affected by muscle mass.



65. Q: Dose of methotrexate in rheumatoid arthritis? 
A: 10–15 mg up to 20 mg once weekly.



66. Q: E. faecalis sensitive to what if imipenem/meropenem are not options? 
A: Vancomycin, ciprofloxacin, moxifloxacin, norfloxacin, all penicillins except penicillin V and procaine, nitrofurantoin, daptomycin, linezolid. 
Note: Besides imipenem.



67. Q: Least anti-anginal effect of beta-blockers? 
A. Decrease force of the heart 
B. Decrease exercise-induced tachycardia 
C. Increase diastolic volume/filling 
D. Decrease systolic fibre contraction 
Answer: C. Increase diastolic volume/filling



68. Q: Best option after oral procedure in penicillin allergy: metronidazole or clindamycin? A: Both can be used.



69. Q: MTX + HCQ combination: hepatotoxicity or no reaction? 
A: No interaction.



Page 7
70. Q: Shingles / Shingrix timing after infection and second dose timing? 
A: After shingles infection: healthy patient after 1 year; immunocompromised patient after 3 months. 
Second dose: healthy patients 2–6 months apart; immunocompromised patients 1–2 months apart.



71. Q: Potassium reabsorption/regulation in kidney? 
A: Potassium is mainly reabsorbed in the proximal tubule, but potassium excretion/regulation is controlled by the collecting duct due to aldosterone.



72. Q: Can ceftriaxone remain stable at high temperature 121°C after sterilisation? A: No. The beta-lactam ring cannot withstand heat.



73. Q: Which one does not begin with inflammation in synovial membrane? 
A) Gout 
B) Cellulitis 
C) Rheumatoid arthritis 
D) Ankylosis 
Answer: B) Cellulitis



74. Q: Which cephalosporin for CAP? 
A: Ceftriaxone. 
Note: If the question asks “least”, choose cefuroxime. Main antibiotics for CAP listed: cefalexin, cefotaxime, ceftriaxone, penicillins, azithromycin.



75. Q: Benzodiazepam contraindicated in depression? 
A: Correct.



76. Q: If a health representative asks a pharmacist to access generic drug data on your computer, what is the appropriate action? 
A. Give a printout of the details 
B. Open the site with the supervisor 
C. Give access without supervision 
D. Do not share any data 
Answer: D. Do not share any data



77. Q: Approached by a service provider for patient confidential information to send in a report. The LEAST thing to do is: 
A. Ask for patient consent 
B. De-identify patient information 
C. Verify provider authenticity 
D. Share only the relevant data 
Answer: D. Share only the relevant data



78. Q: Another HCP contacted you requesting information regarding a patient. Least correct: 1. Take patient consent first 
2. Provide relevant information only 
3. De-identify the information provided 
4. Check the authenticity of HCP 
Answer: 3. De-identify the information provided 
Note: If it is another HCP, do not give any information without proper consent/authority.

A. Days 0–5 of the menstrual cycle 
B. Days 5–9 of the menstrual cycle 
C. Days 10–14 of the menstrual cycle 
D. Days 20–24 of the menstrual cycle 
Answer: B. Days 5–9 of the menstrual cycle 
Note: If the option “days 2–5” is not available, choose days 5–9.



81. Q: Gentamicin-induced ototoxicity may be reduced by: 
A. Aspirin

a) Asthma

87. Q: Which causes first-dose hypotension? 
1. Methyldopa 
2. Metoprolol

Note: Prazosin also causes first-dose hypotension.



88. Q: What dosing regimen is MOST appropriate when starting levothyroxine for a frail elderly patient? 
A. 5 microgram twice daily

89. Q: Drug used for movement symptoms in Lewy body dementia: 
A) Levodopa

92. Q: A healthcare provider calls the pharmacist asking for private medication information for a report. Which one is incorrect?

93. Q: Which drug is MOST likely to cause a clinically significant pharmacokinetic drug interaction with warfarin? 
A. Sodium valproate 
B. Paroxetine 
C. Omeprazole

Answer: D. Fluconazole



94. Q: Which steroid has a markedly different physiological property to the other three? A. Norethisterone 
B. Levonorgestrel 
C. Nandrolone 
D. Megestrol 
Answer: C. Nandrolone



95. Q: We can give patient information by: 
A: Patient consent, health provider, or court order.



96. Q: Which drug commonly causes photosensitivity? 
A. Cyclosporine 
B. Trimethoprim 
C. Diazepam 
D. Haloperidol 
Answer: A. Cyclosporine



97. Q: What is the most appropriate description of dystonia caused by antipsychotics? A. Symptoms happen after a few weeks of taking the medication 
B. Dystonia is reversible after decreasing/stopping the medication 
C. Symptoms occur immediately after taking the medication 
D. Dystonia is not related to antipsychotic use 
Answer: C. Symptoms occur immediately after taking the medication



98. Q: St John’s wort least reacts with: 
Options shown: Fluoxetine, Methadone 
Answer: Fluoxetine



99. Q: WBC nadir after chemotherapy usually occurs around: 
A) 1–2 days 
B) 3–4 days 
C) 6–8 days 
D) 30 days 
Answer in original image: C) 6–8 days 
Corrected note shown later: Nadir = 10–14 days



100. Q: Which drug should be used cautiously in psoriatic arthritis? 
A. Methotrexate 
B. Hydroxychloroquine 
C. Sulfasalazine 
D. Leflunomide 
Answer: B. Hydroxychloroquine



101. Q: Not a symptom in acute otitis externa? 
Options: Dizziness, Itching, Discharge, Deafness 
Answer: Dizziness

102. Q: Not a symptom of otitis media? 
Options: Irritability, Pain, Fever, Tinnitus 
Answer: Tinnitus



103. Q: Which may lead to metabolic acidosis? 
Options shown: Hypoventilation, Hyperchloremia, Hyperkalemia, Hyponatremia 
Answer: Hyperchloremia



104. Q: Which one causes hypothyroidism? 
Options: Lithium, Amiodarone 
Answer: Lithium 
Note: Amiodarone causes thyroid dysfunction, but lithium is more marked for 
hypo/hyperthyroidism. If lithium is not in the choices, amiodarone is second choice.



105. Q: Initial immune response and delayed immune response: 
A: Initial immune response = innate immunity. Delayed immune response = adaptive immunity / T-cell.



106. Q: In bacterial vaginosis, is partner treatment required? 
A: Not required.



107. Q: Which vaccine is not recommended in immunocompromised patients? 
A. Yellow fever 
B. Hepatitis B 
C. Hepatitis A 
D. Meningococcal B 
Answer: A. Yellow fever



108. Q: Lithium causes what renal condition? 
A: Nephrogenic diabetes insipidus.



109. Q: Allergy from cephalexin: what should be checked, tryptase or IgE? 
A: Tryptase.



110. Q: Which of the following is correct about sumatriptan dosing? 
Statements shown: 
1. Maximum dose per day = 600 mg 
2. Take 1 tablet and, if needed, repeat after 8 hours 
3. If no response, take another dose after ≥2 hours 
Corrected notes: 
- Maximum should be 300 mg/day 
- If no response, do not repeat 
- Repeat dose after 2 hours, not 8 hours, only if there was response



Page 12
111. Q: Levothyroxine counselling points: 
A: 
- Take at the same time every day with respect to food. 
- Best absorbed on an empty stomach, 30–60 minutes before breakfast, or at bedtime at least 3 hours after evening meal. 
- Separate from medicines for stomach ulcers, heartburn, calcium, or iron supplements because they may reduce effectiveness. 
- Tell doctor if symptoms of hyperthyroidism occur, e.g., palpitations, excitability, insomnia, flushing, sweating, or weight loss. 
- Ensure the patient understands how to take the medication. 
- For infants, disperse tablets in a small amount of water or non-soy milk.



112. Q: In porphyria, contraindicated drugs: 
A: Thiopental and phenytoin.



113. Q: Cardiac conduction pathway / which is part of conduction? 
A: SA node → AV node → Bundle of His → Bundle branches → Purkinje fibres → ventricles. Note: If choosing between options, choose Purkinje fibres. Bundle of His is the atrioventricular bundle.



114. Q: What should NOT be taken with doxycycline? 
Options: Milk, Zinc 
Answer: Zinc 
Note: Milk/dairy products are okay with doxycycline and minocycline.



115. Q: Retinoids are contraindicated in: 
A: Pregnancy and breastfeeding.



116. Q: Which regulates gene expression? 
Options: mRNA, tRNA 
Answer: mRNA 
Note: tRNA just delivers amino acids.



117. Q: Which statement about amiodarone interactions is NOT correct? 
A. It interacts with levothyroxine and can affect controlled hypothyroidism 
B. It is an inhibitor of CYP3A4/5 
C. Digoxin dose should be reduced to one-third 
D. It causes QT prolongation with venlafaxine 
Answer shown: D. It causes QT prolongation with venlafaxine 
Correction note shown later: If asking “least associated”, answer may be C; venlafaxine with amiodarone was noted as no QT effect in the screenshot.



118. Q: Photosensitivity is NOT an issue with: 
A. Fenofibrate 
B. Doxycycline 
C. Mycophenolate 
D. Cyclosporine 
Answer: A. Fenofibrate

a. Temazepam

123. Q: Atenolol suspension: thixotropic or pseudoplastic? 
A: Suspensions show pseudoplasticity with thixotropy. Both.



124. Q: Which part of the kidney is mainly responsible for potassium reabsorption/secretion regulation? 
A) Glomerulus 
B) Proximal convoluted tubule 
C) Loop of Henle 
D) Distal tubule/collecting duct 
Answer: D) Distal tubule/collecting duct 
Note: About 65% of potassium is reabsorbed in the PCT, but regulation is mainly distal tubule/collecting duct.

133. Q: Which antibiotic has least oral bioavailability? 
Options shown: Aminoglycosides, Vancomycin, Dapsone 
Answer: Aminoglycosides first; vancomycin second. 
Note: Aminoglycosides have no oral route. Vancomycin can be used orally for local action in colitis.
